## 2010 Great Britain National Championship

![](https://i.ormos.uk/31/25/3125de904a867fcd1f0dee9b72846089075c725db04a8302515010db2d46befa.jpg)

Congratulations to Joe Jackson, the 2010 Great Britain National Champion, and his teammates going to Chiba, Richard Bland, Daniel Gardner and Jonathan Randle! 172 players sat down on Friday as Great Britain's National Championships began, but on Sunday only eight remained. There were two past National Champions, in Dan Gardner and Jonathan Randle, and neither disappointed in making it to the final four. It was Joe Jackson and Richard Bland, who had built their deck together the night before the event, who proved the toughest competitors in the final stages though. Bland had lost only a single Standard game with his deck before facing his teammate in the 75-card mirror. It was not to be his day though, as Jackson powered through to victory to become the first Scottish player to hoist high the trophy of Great Britain National Champion.

So ends a fantastic four days of **Magic** here in London, England. We've had just about every format under the sun all under one roof here, and the party atmosphere continues even after the main event has finished. While it may still be months away, Britain's national team are already looking expectantly toward December in Chiba. We'll see you there.

## Quarterfinals – Richard Bland v Eduardo Sajgalik

*by Rich Hagon*

Looking around the top 8 of nationals this year, at least five could reasonably expect to be the leading British finisher at a typical European Grand Prix, and that would certainly be true of both of these players. Sajgalik is in his second GB nationals top 8, having lost to eventual champion Craig Jones in 2007. In the meantime, the globe-trotting Sajgalik has represented Canada at Worlds, having made their team in 2008. As for Bland, his most notable accomplishment came at the gigantic Legacy Grand Prix in Madrid earlier this year, where he fought the good fight all the way to the semi-finals.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6a/ac/6aac53c87d33f4ef7b3867a1f1a4dde30f2dddaa1fd16c51b424c7f30e0ae2f5.jpg)*Sajgalik vs Bland - tried to come up with a funny caption, but could only come up with a bland one.*

Sajgalik opened proceedings, with Bland the first to get a creature onto the battlefield, his [Sylvan Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Ranger) fetching a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains). That was soon joined by [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage). [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) Cascaded into [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse), killing Bland's [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage). [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) piled in, dropping Sajgalik to twelve.

With five mana up, Sajgalik had done little so far, and continued that trend when he simply passed the turn. Bland added a second [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) before turning both sideways. [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) from Sajgalik Cascaded into [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning), leaving Bland a difficult discard decision. After some thought, [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) bit the dust, leaving him [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa), [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), and [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) in hand. Sajgalik fell to seven from the remaining [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine).

That said, he continued to be aggressive with his [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), forcing Bland to chump with his [Sylvan Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Ranger). Sajgalik's singleton [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth) brought him a welcome four point life swing, leaving the scores at eleven to ten in his favor.

[Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) from Bland handily brought him [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage), and that meant more [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) action. Sajgalik had [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth) back, but he faced double [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine), which he manfully took, dropping to just three.

Five mana on his own turn meant [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) from Sajgalik, and when he Cascaded into [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning), it was clear why Bland had kept [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) in his hand, allowing him to discard it and [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan), and leaving him [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) in hand. Now he needed a land to make the Baneslayer cost. He didn't find it, but he found something better, a third [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine). Boy that card's good.

Bland 1 – 0 Sajgalik

[Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) allowed Bland to fast-track [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) on turn two, while Sajgalik opened on a turn three [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax). A dying Thrinax can be a problem, but an [Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exile)d Thrinax isn't, and that's what [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) turned the Thrinax into. With Sajgalik now struggling, Bland added [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar), and then a second [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) to leave Sajgalik in big trouble. Five mana was [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) for the [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), and the Cascade gifted Sajgalik a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech). Once the [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) resolved, Bland went to work with the old [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe) wheeze, activating his Knight to give itself protection from the [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast).

Sajgalik had [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) the following turn to finally deal with the Knight, but at six life he was still under the gun. [Lavaclaw Reaches](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lavaclaw+Reaches) brought him some small measure of blocking prowess, and with one mana open he had enough to allow the Reaches to trade with one of the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s. Bland added [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), and now at just two life, Sajgalik needed something amazing.

He had [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan). Plus friends.

Bland had [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), Cascading into [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), bringing back the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine), and leaving a battlefield team of double [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), double [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine), [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), and [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), facing Sajgalik with [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan), two Zombie tokens, and the [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), which could never be activated. Thanks to the Hierarchs, a single [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) attacked as a 6/5, with Sajgalik losing a Zombie token.

Sajgalik attacked with the [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) to bring him two more tokens, and Bland wasted no time trading [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) for the [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan). The black Mythic, after all, wouldn't be coming back, and the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) probably would be.

Bland discarded [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) to his [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), tutoring up [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan). With [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) Cascading into yet another [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), the third [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) came calling. Sajgalik kept trying, falling to one via [Verdant Catacombs](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Verdant+Catacombs). [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander) brought him three more tokens.

'You'd be shocked if I won this game' said Sajgalik.

'I would be a little bit surprised, I'm not gonna lie' replied the ever-calm Bland.

He found another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) on top of his library, and yet again Cascaded into usefulness with [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). Thanks to [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) he was twenty five to one ahead, but it was the one he was interested in. Five attackers meant five blockers. Time for another Sajgalik miracle. [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) off the top sent three [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) packing.

Time for Bland to stop the foolishness. [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan) arrived. The Knight fetched a land. Bland had three mana. Three mana meant [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage). [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) meant game over.

Bland 2 – 0 Sajgalik.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6c/fc/6cfc2898a9c42aa0ec44aa8e72f3ffd412e37cf90f34243707755202f592356e.jpg)*Bland could do it blindfold, but our budget doesn't stretch to that, so he just closes his eyes.*

So far, things had gone very smoothly for Bland. That changed when he mulliganed to six, and saw his turn one [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) meet a swift [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) from Sajgalik, who also had the hyper-efficient burn spell ready for Bland's next offering, a [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman). Bland's [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) found him [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), but Sajgalik was getting into tokens mode, making a pair of [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), and a [Nest Invader](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nest+Invader).

With the ground clogged, Bland looked to take the battle to the skies, casting the mighty [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel). Sajgalik came back with [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander), continuing the token theme. In came the Baneslayer, and Bland had the answer for the Siege-Gang, with [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) finding a more important target than a [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax).

Using the [Nest Invader](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nest+Invader) token, Sajgalik reached a critical six mana, and that meant [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) (and pals.) For the first time in the match, [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s weren't looking completely dominant. Bland discarded [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) to [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) to rustle up a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), which he promptly cast, Cascading into [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), bringing back the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine).

Just think about that last sentence for a moment. Seriously, how much fun is ***Magic*** right now?

To be fair, you can probably think about it a bit longer, because the players went into mental overdrive, trying to work out what combat was going to look like. Eventually, Sajgalik went into the red zone with (deep breath):

[Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan), Two 2/2 Zombie Tokens, Two 1/1 Goblin Tokens,Two [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax)

...

Four hundred and sixty two blocks and a [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) later, Bland was left with [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), and a life score that read nine to both players. [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) activated, and the [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) Bland searched up Cascaded nicely yet again into [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary).

Nine life each.

One card each.

Seventy three creatures each (approximately).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3d/84/3d84c1e8ae6d36c7acc630661be16442ccb71da1747f2dbe324a435c4dfda8b8.jpg)*Eduardo Sajgalik - he's got all the morphs.*

More super-complicated head-scratching ensued once more, with Sajgalik forcing through three more damage, and dropping a third [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax). Those Thrinax were making life awkward for Bland, who had the creatures to deal with them, but not the flood of tokens they would produce. [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) continued to do the business, converting [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) into [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth), which he swiftly added to the battlefield.

'Six saprolings' were the next words heard, bringing you in two words the significant moments of that particular combat. Bland discarded [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) to fetch [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic), which in turn brought [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) to the party.

With fifteen creatures on the battlefield, things started to shift. For the first time in a while, Sajgalik felt unable to attack. Bland searched up [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage), and the last time I checked, [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) was quite good against 1/1 tokens. [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) plus [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar), meanwhile, is quite good against everything.

Ping – Goblin token gone.

Ping – Zombie token gone.

Bland equipped the [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) to his [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth), and sent it into the red zone. Sajgalik blocked with [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), and avoided lifegain for Bland by shooting his own Thrinax with [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt). Time was running out for Sajgalik. He activated [Lavaclaw Reaches](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lavaclaw+Reaches), and sent the team of doom into battle for what would surely be the final time. Nine tokens joined the [Lavaclaw Reaches](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lavaclaw+Reaches) in Sajgalik's last stand. Bland had blockers for six, leaving four tokens unblocked. Could Sajgalik possibly conjure up six final points of damage?

He could not, and Richard Bland had booked his trip to Worlds.

Richard Bland 3 – 0 Eduardo Sajgalik.

  

## Quarterfinals – Dan Gardner vs Andrew Morrison

*by Tim Willoughby*

Dan Gardner, the current Great Britain National Champion is poised for an unprecedented repeat performance. He snuck into top 8 on tie-breakers, and now that he's in, in spite of any posturing he might make to suggest he's not bothered, he really is. Playing a deck similar to that of his 2009 win, updated by Quentin Martin, Gardner's plan was to stick a [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), and ride it to victory, backed up by blue control and planeswalkers. His opponent, Andrew Morrison of Scotland, piloted the great menace of Jund, the cockroach of the format that just won't die. Morrison had been testing the matchup late into the night, and seemed confident that it would be a good one. This coverage reporter hopes he was right.

Dan had the first play of the best of five match, in [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens), that would prove a fine blocker for [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) from Morrison. Each player's deck was loaded with two for one potential, and Dan was the first to get ahead on cards. Gardner had a second wall, and when [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) hit the red zone he was quick to block. A [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) from Morrison saw Dan use [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) on his own wall to get more lands, effectively letting him catch up on being a land behind from being on the draw. Morrison added a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) to his board.

A [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) on [Rootbound Crag](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rootbound+Crag) from Dan left Andrew without access to green mana, and [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) drew Gardner another card. Morrison wasn't able to get to Jace, and on his swings only got as far as killing off the second [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) after blockers with a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d8/2c/d82cde08e35e1609171b5a2db665ac30c35ec110f4bba413dc4965229de33107.jpg)*Dan Gardner vs Andrew Morrison*

Gardner's planeswalkers plan was furthered with [Elspeth, Knight-Errant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elspeth%2C+Knight-Errant), who made a Soldier token. Morrison simply ignored them, attacking Dan directly to put him on 14, then using [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) to kill Elspeth. Dan killed off his own [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) by using it to draw a third card, and replaced it with [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor). He used the [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) ability, before passing the turn with two mana up.

Morrison found a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) to take him back up to four mana. [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) killed off Jace, while [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) attacked Dan down to 11. That life total went down to 10 following a fetchland activation, but Gardner was hopeful to get it back up with a [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel). Dan had a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) for [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate) from Morrison, but could do nothing about the [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) that followed. He dropped to just 3 on attacks.

Gardner was well on the back foot. He cast a [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens), and [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), who bounced [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech). Morrison wasn't about to replay his Leech though. He cast [Sarkhan the Mad](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sarkhan+the+Mad), and used the planeswalker to sacrifice [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), and make a dragon token. [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) had to bounce that dragon, lest Sarkhan's third ability burned him out. Morrison, for his turn, attacked with his three Saprolings, getting in one damage as [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) and [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) blocked.

For the second time [Sarkhan the Mad](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sarkhan+the+Mad) turned a Saproling into a Dragon. Morrison followed up with [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) and a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) that was hit by a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak). Gardner was on two, and remained in rough shape. He used Jace to [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm), before casting [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity) to draw three more. A [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) drew him yet another card, but it was all for nought, and he scooped up his cards for game 2.

Dan Gardner 0 – 1 Andrew Morrison

Game two saw no spells from either player for the first few turns, though a pair of [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge)s from Gardner looked a little threatening. Morrison seemed unconcerned. His mana base was almost all basics, and he had a land destruction plan too, in the form of [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster). The goblin destroyed Dan's [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade), but not before he'd used it for mana to cast [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) on the hasty 2/1.

Dan used [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) on a [Lavaclaw Reaches](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lavaclaw+Reaches) that was coming in, and was suddenly well down on lands, and all out of white mana sources. Another [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) from Dan meant his hand could not cast any white spells, and [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster) number two from Morrison meant that even casting blue spells was getting tougher.

Ruinblaster #2 took Dan to 14 before he drew a much needed [Glacial Fortress](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glacial+Fortress). There was still no play from Gardner though, who had only been able to play one spell in the game. Andrew cast [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) targeting Dan, which seemed a fine test spell against a hand that might be able to cast spells now. Gardner discarded [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) and [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment). Morrison then attacked with [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster) and [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine). Gardner could not really afford to be throwing away another [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge), putting him back down to three lands, as having the chance to cast [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) was one of the ways for him to get into the game. The threat of that damage was too much though. He used an Edge on the Ravine, and a [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) on [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/31/49/31498be86135e124ad571feee694c5423fa3c5f2c920530d39ff06e3e901ab42.jpg)*Dan Gardner, the current champ looking for the repeat.*

Dan had a brief respite, while Morrison rebuilt. In this time he drew some lands, to open up his options. Morrison cast a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), which found a [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), with the elf hitting a [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel). Dan used back to back copies of [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity) on successive turns, meaning that all his land woes were at an end, and that he could gradually mount a defence, albeit from 8 life. Another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) came along, bringing [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), to which Dan used [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) on [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax). Gardner was down to five, and cast [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment).

Morrison looked at his opponent's graveyard. Both copies of [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) from Gardner's list had been used, the Scotsman noted with a pleased tone. Dan was now on the fight back though. He cast [Sphinx of Jwar Isle](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+Jwar+Isle), and had a look at the top of his deck. He had 2 mana up for a counterspell of some sort, which would likely need to be [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze), as [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) would do little against the 10 lands on Morrison's side of the board.

That Morrison did not have a spell for his turn suggested either mana flood, or a hand with nothing but removal, which would be impotent against the Sphinx. Dan was almost nonchalant in his attacks. With so big a flyer, it wouldn't take him long to close out the game, and he even played a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) to keep him in counter***Magic*** by drawing more cards.

Morrison tried a kicked [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster), but it met with a [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel). [Sphinx of Jwar Isle](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+Jwar+Isle) kept on rumbling in, and when Dan discarded [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) at the end of turn, Morrison looked on in despair. He drew for his turn and scooped up his cards. This quarter-final would not be a sweep.

Dan Gardner 1 – 1 Andrew Morrison

For the third game, Morrison was on the play with a mulligan, while Dan had a full grip, and a rock solid mana base with [Sejiri Refuge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Refuge) and [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) for the first two turns.

Morrison, after a little thought, played [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) on turn three, hitting a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) and a [Glacial Fortress](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glacial+Fortress) from Gardner's hand. Dan rebuild his hand by casting [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens), making the most of the fact that Morrison's start had afforded him time to set up.

A kicked [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster) destroyed [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade), but this time Dan had both defence and lands of each colour to work with, while Morrison had little going on. Another [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) came out for Gardner, and drew him into another land, which he quickly played. Morrison looked vexed. He went through his hand carefully before casting [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). With those walls there, he was likely still not doing much in combat, but he did hit another [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster). The Goblin got hit by [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak).

Dan drew and passed for the turn, ready with a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) to stop [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) from being too much of a blowout in Morrison's favour. Only having one wall meant he did take two from a Ruinblaster, but Gardner's life total was far from concerningly low.

The following turn, Morrison had yet another [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster), putting Gardner back down to five lands. Dan used the mana from the [Glacial Fortress](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glacial+Fortress) that hit the bin to cast [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) on [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). Dan still had enough lands to cast [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity) at end of turn, setting up for [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) on his. Morrison pointed at one of his [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster)s with a raised eyebrow and a cheeky smile. These creatures were not for bouncing. Gardner used Jace to fateseal Morrison, and left a card on top. Morrison was not about to start trying to knock 5 loyalty off Jace, and simply kept going in on Gardner to take him to 12. A [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) was the fate for the planeswalker.

Morrison cast a [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) for his turn, but Gardner was able to do better in terms of creatures, in [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel). Morrison didn't like this, and started dropping more monsters of his own, in [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander) (which itself met a [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge)). Morrison had a [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) for Baneslayer, and attacked Dan down t 10.

Gardner was neither down nor out. He had a second [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) to replace the on that had gone away. The angel did a good job of evening up life totals, which Morrison then tried to undo, with a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into a big set of attacks. The life point swing of [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), combined with a [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) and [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) to block with were enough to make this sort of race a losing proposition.

Morrison fired off a [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), seeing three lands. He attacked in, but couldn't do enough to just die on the swingback.

Dan Gardner 2 – 1 Andrew Morrison

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f9/60/f960dfafffaffe1cddbd31825596f14ed7a71cb24796ba331de929bbd72e5e03.jpg)*Morrison on the offensive.*

If it weren't the top eight, the match would at this point be done. With best of five play at the knockout stage though, Morrison had an extra life to work with. Both players kept the grips for game 4, and Morrison had the start of turn 2 [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) that is frequently the source of Jund's more dangerous openings. Gardner had a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) for the Leech when it swung in and pumped, but was soon staring down a [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) that replaced it. A [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) came from Gardner, who lost his Colonnade to [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster). He did have another [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) though, meaning that he was not going to be taking much in the way of combat damage.

Morrison cast [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech). He snuck through 2 points of damage on attacks and passed. Dan cast [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) and used the [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) ability. Dan's deck was a veritable card drawing machine, so as long as he could stay clear of too much beatdown, he'd be in good shape in the late game. Morrison did not want this to happen, and swung with his whole team, including a [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine), in order to take Dan to six, even with two walls slowing his offence down a little.

[Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) bounced [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), after which Dan played a [Sejiri Refuge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Refuge) and passed. Morrison tried for a [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) but was thwarted by [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze). Morrison sent his team after Dan, again ignoring planeswalkers. Gardner went to 5. [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) then came down for Morrison.

Gardner had an end of turn [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) for [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), and on just five lands he did a little digging with [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor). He found another land, and cast his third [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) before passing. Morrison had a potentially devastating [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) on [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens), which hit a [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) from Gardner, who now had all the blocking options he needed, including a [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) for [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine).

Morrison activated another [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) and ran in, forcing another [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) from Garnder. This left Gardner on 4 lands – rather fewer than he really wanted to really be able to take command of the game. Gardner used Jace's fateseal ability before playing [Elspeth, Knight-Errant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elspeth%2C+Knight-Errant), making a Soldier token, and passing.

With two planeswalkers active, even if Gardner was short on land, he was not short on action. Jace's [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) ability found Dan a fifth land, and he passed, content to sit back on his hand and wait for Morrison to try something.

By now Morrison had drawn all of his [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine)s, and was dangerously close to being able to activate two in a turn. Meanwhile though, Dan's two planeswalkers were each at 6 loyalty, creeping toward ultimate levels. Elspeth making a token got up to 7.

Morrison activated both [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine)s, and attacked in, building his board position by finally being able to kill off a [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) in addition to a Soldier token. Dan dropped to 3, as a [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster) got through. The game was in the balance. Dan played a [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), with mana up to protect it. Morrison played [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), but seeing [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel), [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze), [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), [Sphinx of Jwar Isle](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+Jwar+Isle) and [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) could not have made him very happy. Dan had 2 mana up, so one way or another he would have some sort of counter. If Morrison had some sort of burn spell, that was cheap enough to get around mana leak, then taking [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) would end the game. The length of time Morrison thought though suggested that Morrison's hand was not so well stocked, which would make sense given the amount of fatesealing that Jace had been doing.

He took [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) and cast [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander), paying for [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak). This seemed pretty good, but was not quite as good as Elspeth popped her ultimate, making [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) a thoroughly unfair spell that only killed Morrison's creatures. Attacks from Dan with [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), along with the promise of [Sphinx of Jwar Isle](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+Jwar+Isle) was enough to cause Andrew Morrison to extend his hand.

Dan jumped up cheerily. He had just locked up level 4 status for the Pro Players club by year end.

Dan Gardner wins 3-1

  

## Quarterfinals – Joe Jackson v James Foster

*by Richard Coates*

Joe, who made the Top 8 of Grand Prix Brighton last year, is a PhD student originally from Glasgow, but now lives in Coventry, where he tests with fellow GB Nationals Top 8 player Richard Bland. James, a self-confessed ***Magic*** Online 'grinder', made it into Nationals in the [Last Chance](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Last+Chance) Qualifier on Thursday night. Playing mainly at Eclectic Games in Reading, he is a limited specialist, and was glad of the chance to qualify for Nationals in a 40-card format. Despite being widely considered to be favoured in this matchup, Joe couldn't sleep for nerves last night, having been testing late into the night with teammate, Bradley Barclay.

### Game 1:

Joe won the roll, deciding to play, and after a long time pondering, kept his seven cards. James too kept, but with much less thought. Joe led with a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), as did James. Dropping to 18 after his second Fetchland, Joe then played [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), much to James' relief - a [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) would have been devastating. James too made a [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), but his was powered out by a [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra). Getting his third land type with his third fetchland, Joe played [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence), but opted not to attack with the Knight. [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) was James' next play, and Joe answered with a [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), before attacking with the Linvala. With his [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) and Knight 'Linvalidated' James played the first [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) of the match, and with no blocks from Joe, attacked him down to 12, who fetched a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) at the end of James' turn with [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) invited a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) to join Joe's team, and attacked along with the Linvala, trading with James' Knight. James cast a [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), and attempted to bounce the Linvala, but a [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe), courtesy of [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) kept the Linvala squarely in play. [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) beefed up Linvala's attack and dropped Joe to 7, before being returned to Joe's hand by the Jace. A devastating turn for James, where [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) appeared on the battlefield, was sadly to no avail, as [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe) number two made Joe's [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) unblockable, and the game was shortly over.

Joe Jackson 1 - 0 James Foster

The players sideboarded as follows:

James: 2 [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s and 1 [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos) came out for 1 [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) 2 [Sovereigns of Lost Alara](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sovereigns+of+Lost+Alara)

Joe: [Realm Razer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Realm+Razer), [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth), [Sylvan Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Ranger), [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine), and [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan) came out for 2 [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring), 1 [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence), 1 [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) and 1 [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage)

### Game 2:

Pessimistic about his chances in the match, James took the opportunity to reflect with Joe about ***Magic*** Online, and how to play more than one online event at once, before deciding to play, despite Joe's attempts to dissuade him. While James kept his seven, and led with a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), Joe mulliganed, and could only manage a [Sunpetal Grove](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunpetal+Grove). James brought a [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) onto the battlefield, and Joe matched with one of his own. James attacked with his Pridemage, dropping Joe to 16, and added a [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). Joe's [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) showed up on his turn 3, and James' [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) was swiftly dispatched to the graveyard. Dropping to 11 after a Pridemage attack and a [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest), Joe tried to stem the Bant onslaught with a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine). After [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) shenanigans at the end of Joe's turn, James attacked with his Pridemage and landed a [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) by floating mana through a [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). Joe [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar)ed up his Sparkmage and the Pridemage was destroyed. A topdecked [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe), however, meant James tied up the match, as the unblockable Knight dropped Joe to zero.

Joe Jackson 1 - 1 James Foster

### Game 3:

After a brief interlude while Joe visited the restroom, we moved onto the third game, after James explained to me how one can g o 'more infinite' on ***Magic*** Online. Joe decided to play fikept their hands. For the first time, neither player had a first turn [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), the first play being Joe's [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) on turn 3. James' [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) had a brief visit to the battlefield before a second Sparkmage sent it to the Graveyard. [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) again hit a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), but was quickly matched by James' [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine). A second [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) hit another Birds, but Joe's board was becoming very crowded with high quality creatures. James' board was no less impressive after his next turn, as his [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) and [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) returned a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) to the battlefield, after his [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) dealt with Joe's Knight. An [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) from Joe dealt with the Knight, and the [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) met an end from the Sparkamges, and soon James was scooping up his cards.

Joe Jackson 2 - 1 James Foster

### Game 4:

Lamenting his loss of the die roll, James again decides to play first, as Joe reflects on the mediocre quality of his [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) cascades in the last game. Both players mulliganed to 5, and with heavy sighs on both sides, kept. Joe had a decent start with a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), and James responded with a [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage). A turn 3 [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) for Joe met with a [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) from James, which temporarily bounced the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine). A [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) hit the board for the Bant player, matched with a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) representing Naya, and the [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) hit the bin. James considered his options, and with a final sigh, scooped up his cards.

Joe Jackson 3 - 1 James Foster

Joe Jackson wins 3-1 and advances to the Semi-Finals.

## Quarter Finals – James Cleak vs. Jonathan Randle

*by Richard Moore*

Jonathan Randle is probably best known for being the 2008 Great British national champion and is a regular English presence at the Pro Tour. His opponent, James Cleak, while less known for his **Magic** achievements, excepting his Guildpact pre-release victory, has a PHD in Philosophy and has run the London Marathon.

As the players sat down and began shuffling for the match, both seemed to be reasonably comfortable and confident with the match up. Traditionally, James Cleak's Naya deck would be considered to have the small edge against his counterparts Bant Mythic deck, in no small part due to the [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage), bane of [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) and [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch)s everywhere. However, Randle was reassured by his deck's more reliable mana base and his main deck [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ea/81/ea81d839bc22eef2784df36bcf606266a7c111ea2ef063bc8aa1f15cbf0f16aa.jpg)*James games - it's true and it rhymes*

**Game 1**

Cleak won the dice roll and elected to play, keeping his opening 7. Randle was less happy with his and sent it back but opted to keep the 6. Both players opened with [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) and each had a 2 drop too, [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) for James and [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) for Jonathan. James' third turn produced an [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) which took down the shaman before it could go digging for any troublesome creatures but, with only 1 remaining loyalty, Randle's [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) allowed the [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) (fresh with protection from black courtesy of a [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe)) to fly over and exaltedly cast the plainswalker down.

Cleak moved to the offensive with a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) while Randle sought to solidify his board with a [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) crashed in again and James tried to add the potentially devastating [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) to his army but Randle had the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ready. Jonathan played a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) of his own, electing to hold back once again. James still had plenty of cards in his grip but was still stuck on 3 lands. He attempted to cast his own [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), Randle considered for a few moments and then allowed it to resolve.

Cleak yet again failed to find a land but did find a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) which in turn found a second [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage). James' [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) swung in with protection from green, a fresh [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe) having been found by [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) and Randle, sitting at a lowly 5 life, was forced to put a [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) in its path. With [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) going to work on the 0/1 mana producing team of Randle and his lands being forced to trade with James' creatures, suddenly Randle's board was starting to look a little slim. He had a 2nd Venegevine but it couldnt compare to James' [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel).

James Cleak 1 - 0 Jonathan Randle

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9d/2b/9d2b51f23feeb414d6e7ccde10e76335f7797efc7a4db31c7c11d38f3261e7e6.jpg)*Randle doesn't have Ponder in his deck, ponders anyway*

**Game 2**

Once again both players opened with a turn 1 mana producing creatures. Randle following his up with a turn 2 [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) and James with a [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) seaching up a [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar). This time Randle had [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) ready on turn 3. James opted to swing in with his [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic), planning to cast an [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to finish off the angel if it dared to block but had forgotten that Linvala prevented his [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220526091448/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) from producing mana.
